# Explain Mode — Google Flow scripts

Each film is **twelve separate 8.00-second Google Flow jobs** (96 seconds stitched).
Tell them little — one idea per clip. Fifth-grade voice. Name the color. Say “click.”
Assume they have **never used a website**. Overlay copy = this voiceover.

People tap **Need extra understanding**, then the little play badge beside a tab.

## How to produce

1. Open Google Flow (Veo). Aspect **16:9**. Duration **8 seconds**. Very colorful museum lighting. Giant cartoon cursor.
2. Generate **twelve floating jobs** per tab. Paste **one clip prompt** per job. Do not dump the whole film.
3. The prompt already says: hold the full eight seconds; do not fade at 3s.
4. Record that clip’s VO (slow, one tiny idea), then stitch all twelve in numbered order.
5. Export H.264 MP4 at 1280×720 or 1920×1080. **Do not ship one 3-second clip.**
6. Drop the file at `public/explain-videos/{id}.mp4`. Optional poster `{id}.jpg`. Captions `{id}.vtt` (this script can write them).

**Look:** FIFTH-GRADE FIELD TRIP through ClearPath Trader, 16:9 landscape, exactly eight seconds, no early cutoff. VERY colorful candy-button pills on a dark night sky: electric violet #4D00FF, hot pink #FF1493, lava orange #FF6A00, trophy gold #FFD700, electric cyan #00E5FF, stop-sign red #FF4D4D. Giant friendly cartoon mouse cursor with a big white glove, large enough a child can track it. Huge easy words, one idea on screen. Saturated color wash of THIS clip’s color. Kids science-museum lighting. Soft glitter, not a casino. Slow enough to count “one-Mississippi” eight times.

**Never on screen:** No buy button, no sell button, no order ticket, no P&L fireworks, no countdown clock, no “last chance”, no influencer, no fake breaking-news siren, no invented prices as advice, no confetti checkout, no slot-machine spin, no 3-second jump-cut, no ending the clip before eight seconds.

These films are extra explanation, not trading advice. ClearPath is not a broker.

---

## CEO — CEO dashboard

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `ceo.mp4` |
| Accent | `#FF2E9A` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the hot-pink builder button. |
| Music | Soft pink night-light. Silence between sentences. |
| Captions | One short line per clip. Color the word PINK. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Look at the very top of the screen. Do not look at the middle yet. Up there is a row of colorful buttons, like candy. Stay looking at the top. Do not click yet. Now find the hot-pink button. It says C E O. Pink like bubblegum. The other buttons get darker so this one is easy to see. Most people will not see this pink button. It is only for the person who built the website. If you are visiting, you can skip it. Your mouse is the little arrow on the screen. Slide that arrow onto the pink button. Do not press yet. Just rest the arrow on the pink. Now click. Click means press the left button on your mouse one time. Watch the pink button squish. Then let go. The screen changes. This is a new pink room. Rooms on this website are just different pages. You did not break anything. You will see pink checklists. A checklist is a list of jobs. These jobs are only for the builder, not for you. The teal button saves a backup copy. Backup means a photocopy of important papers, so they are not lost. You do not need this button. This pink room does not buy anything. It does not sell anything. It is not a store. It is the builder’s desk. This room does not change other people’s pictures on the website. Their pages stay the same. Only the builder’s jobs change. If you do not see a pink CEO button, that is normal. Most visitors will not see it. You did not do anything wrong. Click the purple HOME button when you want to keep going. Purple HOME is the front door. Take your time.

_289 words._

## HOME — Home

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `home.mp4` |
| Accent | `#6C5CE7` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the purple front door. |
| Music | Warm welcome hum. No beat drops. |
| Captions | Spell HOME huge. Point at PURPLE. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Look at the top of the screen. That is where the colorful buttons live. If you cannot find a button, look up first. Do not click yet. That row of buttons is how you walk around this website. Each color is a different room. You walk by clicking, not by moving your feet. Find the purple one. It says HOME. Purple like a grape. The little picture on the button is a house. That house means front door. Purple HOME is the front door of the house. A front door is the way back in. You can always find it at the top. Slide your little arrow onto the purple button. The arrow is your mouse. Rest it on the purple. Do not press yet. Now click. Click means press the left mouse button one time. The purple door opens. You did it right. You will see big glowing cards. Some are orange. Some are gold. Some are cyan. Some are pink. They are pictures you can click later. Each card is a door to another room. Under the card is a tiny sentence. Read that sentence before you click. Then click only the card you want. You do not have to click every card. Skipping is allowed. Nothing on this home page spends your money. Looking at cards is free. No credit card is used here. If you get lost later, come back here. Lost just means you do not know which room you are in. Purple HOME finds you. Click the purple HOME button again any time. You can always return to the front door. Take your time.

_270 words._

## Y.W.C. — Y.W.C. — Your World Connected

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `ywc.mp4` |
| Accent | `#FF2E9A` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the lava-pink story room. |
| Music | Warm lava-pink pad. No news horns. |
| Captions | Write Your World Connected once in big letters. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the warm orange-pink button that says Y. W. C. Those are three letters with dots. It lives in the colorful row at the top. Those letters mean Your World Connected. Say it with me: Your. World. Connected. That is the long name for this room. Now click that orange-pink button. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Then wait for the room to open. This room puts stories, magazines, and a little price picture on one page. One page means you can see them together. So you do not have to open ten other websites. Ten websites would be ten different windows. Here they live in one room. See the colorful chips? Those are filters. A filter is a button that hides some stories and shows others. Filters are like sorting crayons by color. Click one chip. Only that color of stories stays on the desk. Then click a story to read it. It opens like a picture book. You can stop reading whenever you want. The little chart on the side is only a picture of prices. A picture cannot make you buy. It is just a drawing of numbers. This room does not tell you what to buy. Stories are not orders. Nobody here is the boss of your money. If the page feels loud or busy, that is okay. Your eyes can get tired. You are allowed to leave. Click the purple HOME button and leave when you want. The front door is always at the top. Take your time.

_256 words._

### Master Flow prompt

## INDACREATOR — INDACREATOR

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `indacreator.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the long-word workshop. |
| Music | Clean cyan. One school-bell ding on compile. |
| Captions | Show Compile and Apply in huge type. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the button that says INDACREATOR. That is a long word. Sound it out slowly: in-da-cre-a-tor. It is cyan, like bright water. It means indicator creator. That is a workshop that draws helper lines on pictures of prices. Helper lines are drawings, not orders. Click the long-word button. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Wait for the workshop room to open. You can drop a file into the box. Or you can paste words. Or you can try the Gold Bar sample. Pick only one way. Then find the button that says Compile. Compile is one word. Rest your little arrow on it. Do not press yet. Now click Compile. Compile means please check this recipe. A recipe is a list of steps. The computer reads it. If the recipe is broken, you will see a red error. That is okay. You did not break the website. Red here means “fix this line,” not “you are in trouble.” A helper named River Genie can fix the words. A helper suggests. You still choose. Then you can click Compile again. When you are ready, click Apply to All Charts. Apply means stick the drawing onto the pictures. It still does not buy anything. Then click the orange CHARTS button to see the drawing on the big picture. Orange is the charts room. This workshop never spends money. Drawing helper lines is free on this page. There is no store here. There is no checkout. Remember the little steps: click the long-word button, click Compile, then click orange CHARTS. Take your time on each step.

_266 words._

---

## CHARTS — Charts

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `charts.mp4` |
| Accent | `#FF7B00` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: orange building-block candles. |
| Music | Soft orange air. Extra slow on candle colors. |
| Captions | Green-ish = finished higher. Red-ish = finished lower. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the bright orange button that says CHARTS. Orange like a sunset. It lives in the colorful row at the top. Click the orange button. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Then wait. The orange room will open. You will see boxes that look like building blocks. They stand in a row. This is a picture of prices over time. Grown-ups call them candles. Each block is one chunk of time. A chunk might be one minute or one day. You do not need that word yet. A green-ish block means the price finished higher in that chunk of time. Higher is just a direction. It is not a command to buy. A red-ish block means the price finished lower in that chunk of time. Lower is just a direction. It is not a command to sell. At the top, click the search box. Type a name if you want a different picture. Letters appear as you type. Then wait for the new picture. The colorful profile buttons change colors so the picture is easier on your eyes. Some people like calm cyan. Some people like fewer lights. Those color buttons do not change the real price. The number stays the same. Only the clothes on the picture change. Drawing tools are like crayons on the picture. You can mark the picture. This is school, not a store. You cannot buy or sell from this page. There is no shop button hiding here. This is a picture room. If the screen feels too busy, try Calm Focus, or click purple HOME. Both are allowed. Take your time.

_270 words._

### Master Flow prompt


## NEWS — News

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `news.mp4` |
| Accent | `#4D6FFF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the blue headline list. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the blue-purple button that says NEWS. Blue like a library stamp. It lives in the colorful row at the top. Click the blue NEWS button. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Then wait for the list to open. This page is a list of real headlines from the internet. A headline is the title of a story. We did not write those titles. Look at the very top. A little light tells you if the list is working, empty, or turned off. Read the light before you read the list. If the list is empty, we did not make up fake stories. Empty means the wire is quiet. Blank is honest. Making up news would be lying. This website does not lie about headlines. If we do not have one, we leave the space blank. Each card shows who wrote it, the date, and a short bit. Who. When. Title. Read those three parts. Click a title if you want the whole story on their website. Their website is a different place. You can come back. Headlines are just information, like a poster on a school wall. You can look. You do not have to do what a poster says. They are not orders. They do not tell you what to buy. A poster is not a shopping list. When you are done reading, you can leave. Done means you have looked enough. You do not have to finish every card. When you are finished with news, click the purple HOME button. That is the front door. Take your time.

_264 words._

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the shiny gold button that says MEMBERSHIPS. Gold like a trophy. It is a long word. It lives in the colorful row. Gold like a trophy, not like a race. A trophy can sit still. You do not have to hurry because the button is gold. Click it. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Looking at this page will not charge a card. This page is a big chart of what each plan includes. A plan is a bundle of tools. The chart is for reading, not for racing. The chart talks about pictures, school lessons, and tools. Those are the kinds of things a plan can include. No prices on screen. Public checkout is turned off. Checkout means the shop desk. Off means you cannot pay here right now. Clicking here will not charge a credit card. You can look. Looking is free. Your card stays in your pocket. You do not have to pick anything. You can just read the columns and leave. Skipping a plan is allowed. The little dots tell you what is really working versus only written on the paper. Working means you can use it today. Paper means it is a plan for later. Gold is just a color. It is not a prize timer. Nothing will expire if you sit and read. Nobody is rushing you. When you are done looking, click Back to the desk. That button takes you to the work rooms without buying. Or click the purple HOME button instead. That is the front door of the website. Take your time. You do not have to pick a plan.

_273 words._

## Explain on — Need extra understanding

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `explain.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the cyan play square and tiny TVs. |
| Music | Cyan shimmer. Long pauses. |
| Captions | Need extra understanding / Explain on. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> See the bright cyan button that looks like a tiny movie play square? Cyan is bright blue-green. That square is a little TV. It says Need extra understanding. Read all four words. They mean “I want a slower movie about this website.” Click it one time. One time means once. Do not double-click. Press the left mouse button and let go. Now the same button says Explain on. On means the help is awake. The words changed because you clicked. Little play badges appear next to every colorful button — like tiny TVs. Each color gets its own tiny TV. Those badges are extra help movies. A help movie shows how a room works. It does not tell you what to buy. Click a tiny play badge — not the big word. The big word opens the room. The little play picture opens the help movie. A big card pops up with a movie, easy words, and one quiz. That card is this extra-understanding room. A quiz here is just practice. You cannot fail your account. A wrong answer does not lock you out. Try again if you want. If the movie is not ready yet, you still get the words. The words say the same thing the movie will say later. Click the same cyan button again to hide the badges. Again means one more click. The tiny TVs go to sleep. You can leave the help on as long as you want. On or off is okay. Take your time.

_252 words._

## PROFILE — Profile

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `profile.mp4` |
| Accent | `#FF2E9A` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the pink locker. |
| Music | Pink night-light. |
| Captions | No real email. No filled password. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Look at the second row of buttons, under the first row. Some buttons live on the second shelf. Tilt your eyes down a little. Find the pink button that says PROFILE. Profile means your locker on this website. Pink like bubblegum. It lives on the second row. Click it. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Wait for the locker room to open. This room is your locker. A locker is a box with your things. Other people have their own lockers. Type the name you want people to see. Type means press letter keys. The letters appear in the box. You can add a photo of your face, or skip the photo. Skip means you do not have to. Both choices are okay. You can write a short bio — two or three sentences about you. Bio means a tiny story. You can leave it blank. You can keep the locker private so strangers do not see it. Private means the curtain is closed. That is allowed. When you are done, click the Save button. Save means keep these words. The computer remembers the name. Saving your name does not buy anything. It does not sell anything. Save is not a store. It only keeps the words. Then click purple HOME or orange CHARTS if you want another room. You can stay in the locker if you are not done. Remember the little steps: open the pink locker, type a name, then click Save. Take your time on each step.

_253 words._

## AFFILIATE — Affiliate

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `affiliate.mp4` |
| Accent | `#FF2E9A` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the pink share-a-link room. |
| Music | Low lava. No cash. |
| Captions | Show /r/CODE as a cartoon. No yachts. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the pink button that says AFFILIATE. Affiliate is a long word. It means a share-a-link room. Pink like bubblegum. Click it. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Wait for the share room to open. This room is for sharing a special link with friends if you want to. If you want to means it is optional. You can skip the whole room. The link looks like slash r slash and then some letters. Those letters are a code. You do not have to memorize them yet. The link stays asleep until you read the rules and click yes. Asleep means it does not work yet. That is on purpose. Read the rules. Click yes. The link wakes up. Yes means you understand the rules. Do not click yes if you did not read. Then you can copy the link. Copy is a button on this page. Click copy one time. Wait for the paper to duplicate. Copy means the computer remembers the words so you can paste them later. Paste means put the words somewhere else. The numbers are just counts, like tally marks on a chalkboard. They are not a video game. There is no high score prize. This room does not buy or sell in the market. Sharing a link is not placing a trade. No shop. If you do not want to share, that is fine. You can leave this room unused. Optional means no one is mad. Click the purple HOME button and leave this share room. The front door is at the top. Take your time. Sharing is optional.

_268 words._

## CLEARPATH CINEMA — ClearPath cinema

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `cinema.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the cyan movie theater. |
| Music | Theatre hush. |
| Captions | No YouTube logo. No fake P&L. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the cyan button that says CLEARPATH CINEMA. Cinema means movie theater. Cyan is bright blue-green. It is a long label. Click it. Click means press the left mouse button one time. The lights will get softer, like a theater. This room is a movie theater inside the website. Seats are a picture. The glowing wall is the screen. You will see rows of pictures, like shelves of movies. Each picture is one movie you can pick. You do not have to pick them all. Click one picture on the shelf. One means only one movie for now. Rest your little arrow on it, then press. A player opens. Player means a box that shows the movie. The box has a play triangle and a bottom bar. You can drag the little bar at the bottom to skip ahead. Drag means press, slide, let go. Skipping is allowed. You can make it full screen so the movie is big. Full screen means the movie fills the whole window. These movies teach. They do not tell you what to buy. Teaching is school. Buying is a store. This is school. If a live show is broken, the page will say so in plain words. Broken means it is not playing. We do not hide that. Watch one movie. One is enough for this visit. You can come back for another later. Pausing is allowed. Then click cyan EDUCATION or orange CHARTS if you want another room. School or pictures. Take your time choosing.

_252 words._

## CLEARPATH EDUCATION — ClearPath education

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `education.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the cyan school. |
| Music | Classroom piano, very low. |
| Captions | No “you are a trader now.” |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Find the cyan button that says CLEARPATH EDUCATION. Education means school. Cyan is bright blue-green. It is a long label at the top. Click it. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Wait for the school hallway to open. This is school inside the website. School means lessons and reading. It is not a store. It is not a broker. You will see subject cards — Crypto, Stocks, Forex — like classroom doors. Each door is one subject. You pick one. Click one subject card. One classroom door is enough for now. The other doors can wait until another day. Then click a lesson that is unlocked. Unlocked means the door is open. Locked means wait — try a different lesson. Read it like a short book. Turn the page when you finish a screen. You can stop in the middle. At the end there is a tiny quiz — just to see if the words made sense. A quiz here is practice. You cannot fail your account. Passing does not mean you should spend money. It only means you understood that page. Understanding is not a shopping license. There are also three libraries for extra reading. A library is a room of books. You can visit later. Do one lesson. Then rest. Rest means stop for a while. Your brain can take a break. That is part of school. If your brain is tired, click the purple HOME button. The front door is at the top. Take your time.

_252 words._

## EXIT — Exit

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `exit.mp4` |
| Accent | `#FF4D4D` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the red stop sign. |
| Music | None. Soft door-close on the last clip only. |
| Captions | Red is a stop sign, not a siren. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Look at the second row of buttons, under the first row. EXIT lives on that second shelf. Tilt your eyes down. The last button is red. It says EXIT. Exit means leave. Red is easy to see on purpose. Red means stop, like a stop sign on the street. You look at a stop sign before you move. Look at EXIT before you click it. Click EXIT only when you want to leave this website for now. For now means today. You can come back later. EXIT logs you out. That means the website forgets you are signed in, like hanging up a phone. The call ends. The phone still exists. It does not delete your name. Delete would mean throw away. EXIT does not throw your locker away. It does not close a bank. If you have a bank somewhere else, that bank stays open. EXIT is only this website’s hang-up. It does not buy or sell anything. There is no last purchase hiding under the red button. Red is not a shop. If you meant to look at pictures, do not click EXIT. Pictures live under orange CHARTS. Red is the wrong button for pictures. Click the orange CHARTS button instead if you wanted pictures. Orange is the picture room. Red EXIT is the wrong button for pictures. If you meant the front door, click purple HOME. HOME is not EXIT. Purple means stay in the house. Red means hang up. Remember: red is stop. Only click it when you are done for now. Take your time. You can sit and think first.

_265 words._

## Literacy OS — Literacy OS

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `literacy.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the cyan notebook desk. |
| Music | Dawn-cyan. |
| Captions | Room chips are the lesson. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> Literacy OS is a notebook desk you open from school. Literacy means reading and writing. OS here just means the desk tools. Click the cyan Literacy OS card. Click means press the left mouse button one time. Wait for the notebook to open. Morning Brief is the first page. It shows what you already saved. Brief means a short look, not a yelling list. It is not a to-do list that yells at you. No red badges counting down. You can read it quietly. The colorful chips are rooms. Each chip is a door on this desk. You click one chip to enter one room. Click Thesis Vault to keep your big ideas. A vault is a safe box. A thesis here means a big idea you want to remember. Click Concept Wiki for words explained in easy language. Wiki means a pile of explain-pages. Hard words become pictures. Click Source Sentinel to notice when a page on the internet changes. Sentinel means a watcher. It watches for differences, not prices to buy. Other rooms on this desk can wait. You do not have to visit every chip today. Waiting is allowed. One room is enough. This notebook desk is school. It is not a store. Saving a note does not buy or sell anything. Save one idea on this desk. Then rest. One idea is enough. Rest means stop for now and come back later. Purple HOME is always at the top if you want out. The front door waits. Take your time.

_254 words._

## Encyclopedia of Finance — Encyclopedia of finance

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `encyclopedia.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the cyan money-words library. |
| Music | Library hush. |
| Captions | Not a shopping list of companies. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> The Encyclopedia of Finance is a library of money words. Encyclopedia means a big book of explanations. Finance means money words. Open it from school. School is the cyan EDUCATION room. The library is one of the extra doors. The list on the left is the index — like the contents page in a book. Index means the list of topics. You pick from the list. Click one topic on the left list. One topic is enough. The other topics can wait on the left until later. Then pick a reading level. Beginner is the easy words. Start there even if you feel grown-up. Easy is allowed. You can climb to a harder reading level later. Later means another day. You do not have to climb today. The middle of the screen is the article, like a chapter. A chapter is one piece of the book. Read it slowly. If a word still feels foggy, open the Scholar Tutor and ask in regular English. Regular English means the words you already know. These cards are for learning. Learning means understanding the money word. It is not a shopping list of companies. They are not a list of companies you must buy. Must buy is an order. This library does not give orders. Read one topic. Then click back. Back means return to the list. You can pick a new topic later. Or click purple HOME if you want the front door. The front door is at the top. Take your time leaving the library.

_254 words._

## Encyclopedia of Indicators — Encyclopedia of indicators

| | |
|---|---|
| Slot / filename | `indicators.mp4` |
| Accent | `#00E5FF` |
| Length | **96 seconds** |
| Logline | Twelve little 8s clips: the teal picture-book of helpers. |
| Music | Quiet teal. |
| Captions | SVG pictures only. |

### Continuous narration (record over the 12-clip stitch)

> The Encyclopedia of Indicators is a picture book of chart helpers. An indicator is a helper drawing. The book shows pictures of those helpers. Helpers have names like R S I or moving average. The names sound fancy. You can still look at the picture first. Open this picture book from school. School is the cyan EDUCATION room. This book is one library door. Use the filters on the left to search, like picking a flavor. A filter hides some cards and shows others. Click one flavor. Click a card. Click means press the left mouse button one time. The card will get bigger so you can read it. You will see a picture, a recipe, how to read it, and what it cannot do. Four parts. Read them in that order if you want. A live-overlay badge means you can stick that helper on CHARTS. Stick means the drawing sits on the picture. It is still not a purchase. A helper only talks about prices that already happened. Already happened means yesterday, or a minute ago — not a promise. A helper cannot promise tomorrow. Tomorrow is not drawn yet. There is no crystal ball on this website. Sticking a helper on a picture still does not buy or sell. The sticker is a crayon. The shop stays closed. Read the “what it cannot do” part. That box is the honest part. Do not skip it even if it feels boring. Then go back to the picture book, or click orange CHARTS. Both are allowed. Take your time choosing.

_260 words._

### Master Flow prompt
